On the convergence of flow map parameterization methods in Hamiltonian systems
Álvaro Fernández-Mora 1 {}^{\mbox{1}}, Àlex Haro 1,2 {}^{\mbox{1,2}}, J. M. Mondelo 3 {}^{\mbox{3}}

Abstract
. In this work, we obtain an a-posteriori theorem for the existence of partly hyperbolic invariant tori in analytic Hamiltonian systems: autonomous, periodic, and quasi-periodic. The method of proof is based on the convergence of a KAM iterative scheme to solve the invariance equations of tori and their invariant bundles under the framework of the parameterization method. Starting from parameterizations analytic in a complex strip and satisfying their invariance equations approximatly, we derive conditions for the existence of analytic parameterizations in a smaller strip satisfying the invariance equations exactly. The proof relies on the careful treatment of the analyticity loss with each iterative step and on the control of geometric properties of symplectic flavour. We also provide all the necessary explicit constants to perform computer assisted proofs.



1.
Introduction

Suppose we have a dynamical system for which we know an invariant
torus exists. A very natural question is whether the invariant
torus persists under perturbations. An answer to this
question came from the pioneering works of Kolmogorov, Arnold,
and Moser
[
Kol54
,
Arn63
,
Mos62
]
on the
persistence of invariant tori under
small enough

perturbations of integrable systems—what is today known as KAM
theory. A key ingredient in the proofs on persistence is the
assumption that the perturbation is
small enough
.
Nonetheless, numerical experiments strongly suggest that persistence
occurs beyond the perturbative regime.

The
small enough
assumption on the perturbation was
dropped in what was originally called KAM theory without
action-angle variables in
[
dlLGJV05
]
, where the authors obtain
theorems for the existence of invariant Lagrangian tori in
Hamiltonian systems. Existence is obtained from the convergence of an iterative
procedure, in a scale of Banach spaces, for the embedding of the
torus satisfying a functional equation. Instead of a small enough
perturbation, what is necessary is an approximate embedding
satisfying a functional equation with an error that is
small
enough
.
The method of proof is based on the ideas of
the parameterization method introduced in
[
CFdlL03a
,
CFdlL03b
,
CFdlL05
]
for invariant manifolds of fixed points.
Similar ideas where used to prove the
existence of, and to compute, normally hyperbolic invariant tori
for quasi-periodic maps in the trilogy
[
HdlL06b
,
HdlL06c
,
HdlL06a
]
. For the parameterization method in a variety of different
contexts see e.g.,

[
FdlLS09
,
HdlLS12
,
LV11
,
CCdlL13
,
CH17
,
HM21
,
GHdlL22
]

and for a survey see
[
HCF+16
]
.
This kind of theorems are known as
a-posteriori
and
require some initial data from where a condition for existence
can be checked in a finite number of computations. In
combination with rigorous numerical techniques, it is possible to
perform computer assisted proofs. This approach led in

[
FHL17
]
to the formulation of a general methodology
for such kind of proofs—using the parameterization method—for
Lagrangian invariant tori. A modified approach that leads to
sharp estimates for the condition for existence was introduced in

[
Vil17
]
and similar ideas for systems with extra first
integrals were developed in
[
FH24
]
.

In this work, we focus on tori invariant under vector fields, for
which an extended approach is to look for codimension one tori
invariant under flow maps. One option is to solve numerically the
invariance equation with collocation methods
[
GM01
]
.
However, this approach suffers from the large matrix problem,
which restricts the dimension of the invariant tori by the
computational cost of iterative steps. Another option is to use
Floquet theory to compute reducible invariant tori and avoid the
large matrix problem
[
GJNO22
]
. Under the parameterization
method, efficient algorithms were introduced in
[
HM21
]
to
compute invariant tori for autonomous systems in problems of
celestial mechanics. The method was later generalized to the
quasi-periodic setting in
[
FMHM24
]
.

We build on
[
HM21
,
FMHM24
]
and obtain an

a-posteriori
theorem for partly hyperbolic invariant tori
and their invariant bundles in quasi-periodic Hamiltonian
systems. We use flow maps related to the
natural frequencies of tori to reduce the dimension of the tori
by one and do not use an autonomous reformulation by introducing
fictitious
action
coordinates—this point of view allows
the theorem to be applied to both autonomous and quasi-periodic
Hamiltonian systems and constitutes the proof on the convergence
of the flow map methods of
[
HM21
,
FMHM24
]
. Additionally, we
obtain both the stable and unstable bundles simultaneously which
provides a clear geometrical picture of the tangent space on the
torus. The proof relies on geometric properties of symplectic flavour
that hold approximately when we have parameterizations that
satisfy their invariance equations approximately. We obtain
approximate geometric lemmas for such properties that, together
with the lemma on quadratically small averages obtained in

[
FMHM24
]
, control the error in the KAM iterative procedure.
The new error in the invariance equations is controlled by
explicit constants which require the
careful treatment of the analyticity loss with each iterative
step. The proof concludes by obtaining conditions for convergence
of the KAM iterative procedure.

The structure of this work is the following. In Section

2
we introduce the symplectic and analytic
setting as well as some preliminary notions.
Section
3
describes partly hyperbolic
invariant tori, the iterative procedure for their computation,
and our main result: the KAM theorem. In Section
4

we state and prove the approximate geometric lemmas that we use
to control the new errors after one step of the iterative
procedure; for which we obtain estimates in Section
5
. Lastly, in Section
6
we
conclude the proof of the KAM theorem and in Appendix

A
we provide tables with all the explicit
constants obtained in our estimates.

2.
Setting

2.1.
Basic notation

We consider the vector spaces
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
and

{\mathbb{C}}^{m}
equipped with
the norm

|v|=\max_{i=1,\dots,m}|v_{i}|,

which induces the following norm in the spaces of
n_{1}\times n_{2}

matrices with components in
{\mathbb{R}}
or
{\mathbb{C}}

|M|=\max_{i=1,\dots,n_{1}}\sum_{j=1,\dots,n_{2}}|M_{ij}|.

We denote by
M^{\!\top\!}
the transpose of the matrix
M
. Also,
the
n\times n
identity and zero matrices are denoted by
I_{n}

and
{\mathcal{O}}_{n}
, respectively. Meanwhile, the
n-
dimensional zero
vector is denoted by
0_{n}
. When it is clear from the context,
empty blocks denote zero matrices of suitable dimensions.
Let
{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}={\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{\ell}
be the standard
\ell-
dimensional
torus.
For
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
, we define the
rotation map
{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}:{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
as

{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}(\varphi)=(\varphi+\alpha)\ \mod 1.

Similarly, for
(\omega,\alpha)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d+\ell}
, we define the
rotation map
{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}
as

(2.1)

{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}(\theta,\varphi)=(\theta+\omega,\varphi+\alpha)\ \mod 1.

Throughout the exposition,
(\omega,\alpha)
will be fixed and,
occasionally, we will use the notation
\bar{\theta}:=\theta+\omega
, and
\bar{\varphi}:=\varphi+\alpha
.

2.2.
Symplectic setting

We assume we have an exact symplectic form
\boldsymbol{\omega}
, defined on an
open set
U\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
, with matrix representation

\Omega:U\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times 2n}
. We also assume we have an analytic
Hamiltonian
H:U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}
that depends on time
quasi-periodically as
H(z,\varphi)=H(z,\hat{\alpha}t)
for frequencies

\hat{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
. We can then construct

X:U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
as

X(z,\varphi):=\Omega(z)^{-1}{\rm D}_{z}H(z,\varphi)^{\!\top\!},

and on
U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
, we can define
the vector field
\tilde{X}:U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
as follows

(2.2)

\begin{pmatrix}\dot{z}\\
\dot{\varphi}\end{pmatrix}=\tilde{X}(z,\varphi):=\begin{pmatrix}X(z,\varphi)\\
\hat{\alpha}\end{pmatrix}.

We can think of
U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
as a bundle with base

{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
and of
\tilde{X}
as a fiberwise Hamiltonian vector
field—in each fiber, we have a symplectic vector
structure given by
\Omega
and a Hamiltonian vector field

X(\cdot,\varphi):U\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
. Our goal is to find partly
hyperbolic invariant tori for the vector field
\tilde{X}
.

Let us denote the flow associated to
\tilde{X}
by

\tilde{\phi}:D\subset{\mathbb{R}}\times U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}\to U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
, where
D
is the open set domain of definition
of the flow. That is, for every
(z,\varphi)
let

I_{z,\varphi}\subset{\mathbb{R}}
be the maximal interval of existance of

\tilde{\phi}(\cdot,z,\varphi)
, then

D=\{(t,z,\varphi)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}~|~t\in I_{z,\varphi}\}.

Notice that
\tilde{\phi}
adopts the form

\tilde{\phi}(t,z,\varphi)=\begin{pmatrix}\phi(t,z,\varphi)\\
\varphi+\hat{\alpha}t\end{pmatrix},

where the evolution operator
\phi
satisfies

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\phi(t,z,\varphi)=X\big(\phi(t,z,\varphi),\varphi+\hat{\alpha}t\big),\quad\phi(0,z,\varphi)=z.

From this point onwards, we will adopt the standard notations

\tilde{\phi}_{t}(z,\varphi)=\tilde{\phi}(t,z,\varphi)
and
\phi_{t}(z,\varphi)=\phi(t,z,\varphi)
.

Since the symplectic form is exact,
\boldsymbol{\omega}={\rm d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}
, where
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
is the
action 1-form. Then, the matrix representation of
\boldsymbol{\omega}
is given
by

\Omega(z)={\rm D}a(z)^{\!\top\!}-{\rm D}a(z),

where
a:U\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
and
a(z)^{\!\top\!}
is the matrix
representation of
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
at
z
. Also, because
\boldsymbol{\omega}
is exact
symplectic,
\phi_{t}
is a fiberwise exact symplectomorphism.
That is, for each
\varphi\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
and fixed
t
,
\phi_{t}
satisfies symplecticity

{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{t}(z,\varphi)^{\!\top\!}\Omega\big(\phi_{t}(z,\varphi)\big){\rm D}_{z}\phi_{t}(z,\varphi)=\Omega(z)

and exactness

a\big(\phi_{t}(z,\varphi)\big)^{\!\top\!}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{t}(z,\varphi)-a(z)^{\!\top\!}={\rm D}_{z}p_{t}(z,\varphi),

for some primitive function
p_{t}:U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, see

[
FMHM24
]
for details.

Let us also assume we have an almost-complex structure
\boldsymbol{J}
on

U
, compatible with the symplectic form, with matrix representation

J:U\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times 2n}
. That is,
\boldsymbol{J}
is
anti-involutive and symplectic—in coordinates, these properties
read

J(z)^{2}=-I_{2n},\quad J(z)^{\!\top\!}\Omega(z)J(z)=\Omega(z).

The almost complex structure and the symplectic form induce a
Riemannian metric
\boldsymbol{g}
with matrix representation

G:U\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times 2n}
constructed as
G(z)=-\Omega(z)J(z)
.

A particular example of a compatible tripple
(\boldsymbol{\omega},\boldsymbol{J},\boldsymbol{g})
is
when
\boldsymbol{\omega}
is the standard symplectic form, i.e.,

\Omega_{0}(z)=\begin{pmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{n}&-I_{n}\\
I_{n}&{\mathcal{O}}_{n}\end{pmatrix},

\boldsymbol{g}
is the Euclidean metric, and
\boldsymbol{J}
is given by the
standard linear complex structure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
coming from the
complex structure on
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
. That is,

G_{0}(z)=\begin{pmatrix}I_{n}&{\mathcal{O}}_{n}\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{n}&I_{n}\end{pmatrix},\quad J_{0}(z)=\begin{pmatrix}{\mathcal{O}}_{n}&-I_{n}\\
I_{n}&{\mathcal{O}}_{n}\end{pmatrix}.

2.3.
Analytic setting

We assume we have a real analytic Hamiltonian. Therefore,
we will work with real analytic functions defined in
complex neighborhoods of real domains. Let us construct the
complex neighborhood of
{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
as the following complex strip of
width
\rho

{\mathbb{T}}_{\rho}^{\ell}=\{\theta\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\ell}/{\mathbb{Z}}^{\ell}:\left|{{\rm Im}\ \theta}\right|<\rho\}.

Furthermore, assume we have a holomorphic extension

{K_{0}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d}_{r}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}_{r}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}
of a map

{K_{0}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
, for some
d
and
r>0
,
where we abuse notation and denote both the map and its
holomorphic extension by
{K_{0}}
.
Let us then construct the sets
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}
,

{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}_{r}
, and
{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}_{\varphi}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}
as

(2.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle:=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}:\exists(\theta,\varphi)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}_{r}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}_{r}:\left|{z-{K_{0}}(\theta,\varphi)}\right|<R\},

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}:

\displaystyle=\{(z,\varphi)\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}_{r}:\exists\theta\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}_{r}:\left|{z-{K_{0}}(\theta,\varphi)}\right|<R\},

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}_{\varphi}:

\displaystyle=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}:\exists\theta\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}_{r}:\left|{z-{K_{0}}(\theta,\varphi)}\right|<R\},

for some
R>0
. For analytic functions
f:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
, we consider the
norm

(2.4)

\|{f}\|_{\mathcal{U}}=\sup_{z\in{\mathcal{U}}}|f(z)|,

that extends naturally
for matrix-valued maps
M:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
, with

M_{ij}
analytic, as

(2.5)

\|{M}\|_{\mathcal{U}}=\max_{i=1,\dots,n_{1}}\sum_{j=1,\dots,n_{2}}\|{M_{ij}}\|_{\mathcal{U}}.

For their derivatives, we define the norms

(2.6)

\|{{\rm D}^{k}f}\|_{\mathcal{U}}=\sum_{l_{1},\dots,l_{k}}\left\|{\frac{\partial^{k}f}{\partial z_{l_{1}}\dots\partial z_{l_{k}}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{U}},\quad\|{{\rm D}^{k}M}\|_{\mathcal{U}}=\max_{i=1,\dots,n_{1}}\sum_{j=1,\dots,n_{2}}\|{{\rm D}^{k}M_{ij}}\|_{\mathcal{U}},

where each index
l_{1},\dots,l_{k}
goes from 1 to
2n
.
Furthermore, the action of the

k-
order derivative on
v_{1},\dots,v_{k}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{2}}

satisfies the bounds

\|{{\rm D}^{k}M(z)[v_{1},\dots,v_{k}]}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}\leq\|{{\rm D}^{k}M}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}\left|{v_{1}}\right|\dots\left|{v_{k}}\right|,

where

\displaystyle\left({\rm D}^{k}M(z)[v_{1},\dots,v_{k}]\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle={\rm D}^{k}M_{ij}(z)[v_{1},\dots,v_{k}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l_{1},\dots,l_{k}}\frac{\partial^{k}M_{ij}}{\partial_{z_{l_{1}}},\dots,\partial_{z_{l_{k}}}}(z)\big(v_{1}\big)_{l_{1}},\dots,\big(v_{k}\big)_{l_{k}}.

Lastly, for analytic functions
f:{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
we consider the norm

\|{f}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}=\sup_{\varphi\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}_{r}}\|{f(\cdot,\varphi)}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}_{\varphi}}

and the analogous norm to (
2.5
) for matrix-valued maps.

2.3.1.
Spaces of quasi-periodic real-analytic functions

Let us denote by
{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})
the Banach space of
real-analytic functions
f:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho}\to{\mathbb{C}}
—such that

f({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell})\subset{\mathbb{R}}
—that can be
continuously extended to the closure
\bar{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho}

and endowed with the norm

\|{f}\|_{\rho}=\sup_{(\theta,\varphi)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho}}|{f(\theta,\varphi)}|.

We will also use the notation
{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})^{m}
for
the analogous space for functions
f:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{m}
,
and the natural extension of the norm for functions

M:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}

\|{M}\|_{\rho}=\max_{i=1,\dots,n_{1}}\sum_{j=1,\dots,n_{2}}\|{M_{ij}}\|_{\rho}.

For the derivatives of quasi-periodic real-analytic functions, we
use Cauchy estimates. For any
\delta\in(0,\rho)
, the derivative
operator

\partial_{i}:{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})\to{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho-\delta})

satisfies the bounds

\|{\partial_{i}f}\|_{\rho-\delta}\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\|{f}\|_{\rho},\quad\|{{\rm D}_{\theta}f}\|_{\rho-\delta}\leq\frac{d}{\delta}\|{f}\|_{\rho},\quad\|{{\rm D}_{\varphi}f}\|_{\rho-\delta}\leq\frac{\ell}{\delta}\|{f}\|_{\rho}.

Additionally, the Fourier representation of
f
is given by

f({\theta,\varphi})=\sum_{(k,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d+\ell}}\hat{f}_{jk}e^{\boldsymbol{i}2\pi(k\theta+j\varphi)},

where
\hat{f}_{jk}\in{\mathbb{C}}
and

\hat{f}_{00}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}}f({\theta,\varphi})d\theta d\varphi

is the average of
u
that we denote as
{\langle{f}\rangle}
.
Lastly, for functions
f:{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
and

M:{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
, we define the analogous norms to
(
2.4
), (
2.5
), and (
2.6
).

2.4.
Cohomological equations in
{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}

We dedicate this section to two types of cohomological equations that
naturally arise in KAM theory. Albeit the theory is well known,
see e.g.
[
Rüs75
]
, we tailor the exposition to be well
suited for our purposes.

2.4.1.
Non-small divisors cohomological equations

For
a,b\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that
|{a}|\neq|{b}|,~\omega\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
and
\alpha\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
, we define the cohomological operator

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega\alpha}^{ab}:{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})\to{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})

acting on functions
\xi:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho}\to{\mathbb{C}}
as

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega\alpha}^{ab}\xi:=a\xi-b\xi{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu},

where
{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}
is defined as in (
2.1
). We also define the Rüssmann operator

\mathfrak{R}_{\omega\alpha}^{ab}:{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})\to{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})

acting on functions
\eta:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho}\to{\mathbb{C}}

as the solution of the cohomological equation

(2.7)

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega\alpha}^{ab}\xi=\eta.

That is,
\xi=\mathfrak{R}_{\omega\alpha}^{ab}\eta
solves (
2.7
).
Expanding
\xi
and
\eta
in their Fourier series, we obtain the
converging expansion

\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\omega\alpha}^{ab}\eta\right)({\theta,\varphi})=\sum_{(k,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d+\ell}}\hat{\xi}_{jk}e^{\boldsymbol{i}2\pi(j\theta+k\varphi)},\quad\hat{\xi}_{jk}=\frac{\hat{\eta}_{jk}}{a-be^{\boldsymbol{i}2\pi(j\omega+k\alpha)}}.

Additionally, we have the estimate

(2.8)

\|{\xi}\|_{\rho}\leq\frac{1}{\big||{a}|-|{b}|\big|}\|{\eta}\|_{\rho}.

We will also use the notation
\mathfrak{L}_{\omega\alpha}^{ab}={\mathfrak{L}}^{ab}
and

\mathfrak{R}_{\omega\alpha}^{ab}={\mathfrak{R}}^{ab}
, when it is clear from the context.

2.4.2.
Small divisors cohomological
equations

We also define the cohomological operator

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega\alpha}:{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})\to{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})

as

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega\alpha}\xi:=\xi-\xi{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu},

and for any
\delta\in(0,\rho]
, we define the Rüssmann
operator

\mathfrak{R}_{\omega\alpha}:{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})\to{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho-\delta})

as the zero-average solution of

(2.9)

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega\alpha}\xi=\eta-{\langle{\eta}\rangle}.

That is
\xi=\mathfrak{R}_{\omega\alpha}\eta
solves (
2.9
) with

{\langle{\xi}\rangle}=0.
Again, expanding
\xi
and
\eta
in their Fourier
series, we obtain the formal expansion

\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\omega\alpha}\eta\right)({\theta,\varphi})=\sum_{(k,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d+\ell}}\hat{\xi}_{jk}e^{\boldsymbol{i}2\pi(j\theta+k\varphi)},\quad\hat{\xi}_{jk}=\frac{\hat{\eta}_{jk}}{1-e^{\boldsymbol{i}2\pi(j\omega+k\alpha)}},

where the series is uniformly convergent in a narrower strip if

(\omega,\alpha)
is Diophantine.

Definition 2.1
.

The rotation vector
(\omega,\alpha)\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}
is Diophantine if
there exists
\gamma>0
and
\tau\geq d+\ell

such that for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
(j,k)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\times{\mathbb{Z}}^{\ell}\setminus\{0\}

|j\cdot\omega+k\cdot\alpha-n|\geq\frac{\gamma}{\left(|j|_{1}+|k|_{1}\right)^{\tau}},

where
|\cdot|_{1}
is the
\ell^{1}
-norm.

Then, we have Rüsmann estimate

(2.10)

\|{\xi}\|_{\rho-\delta}\leq\frac{c_{\mathfrak{R}}}{\gamma\delta^{\tau}}\|{\eta}\|_{\rho},

where
c_{\mathfrak{R}}
is the Rüssmann constant. See

[
Rüs75
]
for details. We will also use the notations

\mathfrak{L}_{\omega\alpha}={\mathfrak{L}}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{\omega\alpha}={\mathfrak{R}}
.

3.
Partially hyperbolic invariant tori

We are interested in
(d+\ell+1)-
dimensional tori with
frequencies
\hat{\omega}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d+1}
and
\hat{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
—
invariant for the vector field (
2.2
)—and in their
invariant bundles. Equivalently, for

\hat{\omega}=:\frac{1}{T}(\omega,1)
and
\alpha:=\hat{\alpha}T
, where

\omega\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
and
\alpha\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
, we can look for

(d+\ell)-
dimensional tori
{\mathcal{K}}
, invariant for the flow map

\phi_{T}
, and in bundles
{\mathcal{W}}
with base
{\mathcal{K}}
, invariant under
the differential of
\phi_{T}
, see

[
FMHM24
,
HM21
]
. In the present work, we focus on invariant tori with
rank 1 bundles—which corresponds to
d=n-2
. This choice is
motivated by applications in celestial mechanics such as
quasi-periodic perturbations of restricted three body problems.
In the spirit of the parameterization method, we are
interested in fiberwise embeddings
K:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to U

and
{W}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
, with rate
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\{1,-1\}
,
satisfying the following invariance equations

(3.1)

\displaystyle\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})-K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}=0,

(3.2)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){W}-{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda=0,

where
(K,\mathrm{id})
stands for the following bundle map over
the identity

\displaystyle(K,\mathrm{id}):\quad{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}

\displaystyle({\theta,\varphi})

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big(K({\theta,\varphi}),\varphi\big).

Without loss of generality, we can assume
0<|{\lambda}|<1
.
We will refer to
K
and
W
as the parameterizations of
{\mathcal{K}}

and
{\mathcal{W}}
, respectively. It can be shown that due to certain
geometric properties, see
[
FMHM24
]
, if
(K,{W},\lambda)

satisfy (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), then there exists a
set of coordinates given by a frame
{P}
such that the
differential of the flow map reduces to an upper triangular
matrix-valued map—constant along the diagonal. That is, the
linearized dynamics in the coordinates given by
{P}
reduce to

{\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.65}{${P}$}}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
as

(3.3)

({P}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{-1}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){P}={\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.65}{${P}$}}},

where

(3.4)

{\Lambda_{\scalebox{0.65}{${P}$}}}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}\Lambda&S\\
\hline\cr&\raisebox{0.0pt}[9.90276pt][0.0pt]{$\Lambda^{-{\!\top\!}}$}\end{array}\right),\quad\Lambda:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{n-1}&\\
\hline\cr&\lambda\end{array}\right),

and
S:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
. In Section

4
we provide expressions and bounds for the
geometric properties when (
3.1
) and (
3.2
)
only hold approximately.

3.1.
Adapted frames

In this section we briefly cover the construction of adapted
frames that reduce the linearization of
\phi_{T}
on
{\mathcal{K}}
to an
upper triangular matrix-valued map, constant along the diagonal.
In what follows, assume
{\mathcal{K}}
is an invariant torus under

\phi_{T}
,
{\mathcal{W}}
is the invariant bundle with base
{\mathcal{K}}
, and that
the dynamics in the hyperbolic linear subspace are reduced to a constant
contraction with rate
|{\lambda}|
. For a detailed
description of the construction of adapted frames and their
geometric properties see
[
FMHM24
]
.

We construct a frame
{P}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times 2n}
as
the juxtaposition of two subframes

{L},{N}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times n}
, i.e.,

{P}=\big({L}\quad{N}\big).

The subframe
{L}
is tangent to the torus whereas the
normal subframe
{N}
complements
{L}
to a full basis of

T_{\mathcal{K}}U
, i.e., the tangent bundle of
U
restricted to
{\mathcal{K}}
.
We proceed by first constructing the subframe

{L}
as follows

{L}:=\Big({\rm D}_{\theta}K\quad\mathcal{X}\quad W\Big),

where

\mathcal{X}:=X{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})-{\rm D}_{\varphi}K\hat{\alpha},

and the subframe
{N}
as

{N}=\hat{N}+{L}{A},

for certain
{A}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
, where

(3.5)

\displaystyle\hat{N}

\displaystyle={J}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{L}{B},

\displaystyle{B}

\displaystyle=G_{L}^{-1},

\displaystyle G_{L}

\displaystyle={L}^{\!\top\!}G{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{L}.

The role of
{A}
is to obtain simultaneously the stable and
unstable bundles of the invariant torus but its use is not
necessary, i.e., we could use the frame
{\hat{P}}=({L}\ \hat{N})
. We will
come back to this topic but first let us emphasize some
properties of these matrix-valued maps. The frame
{P}
is
symplectic with respect to the standard symplectic form, i.e.,

{P}
satisfies

{P}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{P}={\Omega_{0}},

from where we obtain

(3.6)

{P}^{-1}=-{\Omega_{0}}{P}^{\top}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}.

Moreover,
{A}
needs to be symmetric to preserve the
symplecticity of
{P}
and
G_{L}
is invertible since
K
is a
fiberwise embedding. That is, for each
\varphi\in{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}
,

K(\cdot,\varphi):{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\to U
is an embedding. Lastly, both
G_{L}

and
{B}
are symmetric.

To see how we obtain both the stable and unstable bundles with
the right choice of
{A}
, let us denote the torsion of the normal bundles
parameterized by
\hat{N}
and
{N}
, when transported by

{{\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})
, by the matrix-valued maps

\hat{S},S:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}

(3.7)

\displaystyle\hat{S}

\displaystyle:=\hat{N}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})\hat{N},

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle:={N}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){N},

where a computation reveals

(3.8)

S=\hat{S}+\Lambda{A}-{A}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\Lambda^{-{\!\top\!}}.

For the details see
[
HM21
,
FMHM24
]
.
From (
3.3
) and (
3.4
), observe that
if we choose
{A}
such that
S
adopts the form

(3.9)

S=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}{\mathcal{S}}&\\
\hline\cr&\end{array}\right),

where
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{(n-1)\times(n-1)}
,
then the last column of
{P}
, that we denote by

\widetilde{W}
, satisfies

(3.10)

{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})\widetilde{W}-\widetilde{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda^{-1}=0.

Therefore,
{W}
and
\widetilde{W}
parameterize both
the stable and unstable bundles of
{\mathcal{K}}
, respectively.

Remark 3.1
.

When
K
parameterizes an invariant torus and the bundle is
invariant, the torsion
\hat{S}

satisfies the symmetry condition

(3.11)

\Lambda^{-1}\hat{S}-\left(\Lambda^{-1}\hat{S}\right)^{\!\top\!}=0.

Then, for
S
as in (
3.9
) and a suitable
symmetric
{A}
, (
3.8
) holds.
However, the symmetry condition for
\hat{S}

only holds approximately when
(K,{W},\lambda)
satisfy (
3.1
)
and (
3.2
) approximately, see Section
4.8
.

Let us define splittings for
\hat{S}
and
{A}
into blocks of sizes

(n-1)\times(n-1),(n-1)\times 1,1\times(n-1),1\times 1
as

(3.12)

\hat{S}=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{S}_{1}&\hat{S}_{2}\\
\hat{S}_{3}&\hat{S}_{4}\end{pmatrix},\quad{A}=\begin{pmatrix}A_{1}&A_{2}\\
A_{3}&A_{4}\end{pmatrix}.

Then, condition (
3.9
) reads

(3.13)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle=\hat{S}_{1}+\phantom{\lambda}A_{1}-A_{1}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu},

(3.14)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\hat{S}_{2}+\phantom{\lambda}A_{2}-A_{3}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda^{-1},

(3.15)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\hat{S}_{3}+\lambda A_{3}-A_{2}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu},

(3.16)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\hat{S}_{4}+\lambda A_{4}-A_{4}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda^{-1},

where we can take
{A}_{1}={\mathcal{O}}_{n-1}
—consequently
{\mathcal{S}}=\hat{S}_{1}
—and
solve (
3.16
) as described in Section

2.4.1
. Notice that (
3.14
) and
(
3.15
) are coupled non-small cohomological equations
that can be rewritten as

(3.17)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left(\hat{S}_{2}\lambda+\hat{S}_{3}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda^{-1}\right)+A_{2}\lambda-A_{2}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega\alpha}^{{\!\>\circ\!\>}2}\lambda^{-1},

(3.18)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left(\hat{S}_{3}+\hat{S}_{2}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\right)+\lambda A_{3}-\lambda^{-1}A_{3}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega\alpha}^{{\!\>\circ\!\>}2},

where
{\mathcal{R}}_{\omega\alpha}^{{\!\>\circ\!\>}2}
stands for
{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}
. Then, we can solve
(
3.17
) and (
3.18
) as
non-small divisors cohomological equations. We will also refer to
{\mathcal{S}}

as the reduced torsion or the
shear
.

Remark 3.2
.

From (
3.11
), (
3.17
) and
(
3.18
), it follows that
{A}_{2}={A}_{3}^{\!\top\!}
.
Consequently,
{A}
is symmetric.

Remark 3.3
.

From (
3.13
), it is possible to further reduce

{\mathcal{S}}
to a constant matrix
{\mathcal{S}}={\langle{\hat{S}_{1}}\rangle}
by solving

{\mathfrak{L}}A_{1}={\langle{\hat{S}_{1}}\rangle}-\hat{S}_{1}.

3.2.
Iterative procedure

Let us define the error in the torus and bundle invariance
equations
{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}},{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
as

(3.19)

\displaystyle{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}=\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})-K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu},

(3.20)

\displaystyle{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}={\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){W}-{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda.

The iterative scheme designed in
[
FMHM24
]

constructs sequences for
K,{W},
and
\lambda
such that for the
limiting objects
K_{\infty},{W}_{\infty}
, and
\lambda_{\infty}
the
errors satisfy
\|{E_{K_{\infty}}}\|=0
and

\|{E_{{W}_{\infty}}}\|=0
in some suitable norm. The sequences
are obtained recursively by choosing corrections

{\Delta K},{\Delta{W}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
, and
{\Delta\lambda}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that,
for
\bar{K}=K+{\Delta K}
,
\bar{W}={W}+{\Delta{W}}
, and
\bar{\lambda}=\lambda+{\Delta\lambda}
,
(
3.19
) and (
3.20
) vanish at first order.
In this section, we describe non-rigorously one step of the
iterative scheme—the rigorous description is postponed to
Section
6.1
and the convergence of the
scheme to Section
6.2
.

3.2.1.
One step on the torus

Let us begin with (
3.19
). We compute the correction of
the torus parameterization in the coordinates given by
the frame
{P}
. That is, we choose
{\Delta K}={P}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}
, where

\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
satisfies

(3.21)

{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})P\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}-P{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}=-{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}

up to first order in
{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}
.
Let us left-multiply the linearized equation (
3.21
) by the
approximate inverse of
{P}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}
given by

-{\Omega_{0}}{P}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}}\mspace{1.5mu}
, see (
3.6
). Then, we
obtain after neglecting higher order terms

(3.22)

\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}\Lambda&S\\
\hline\cr&\raisebox{0.0pt}[9.90276pt][0.0pt]{$\Lambda^{-{\!\top\!}}$}\end{array}\right)\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}},

where

\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}={\Omega_{0}}{P}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}}\mspace{1.5mu}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}.

We can now split
\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}
and
\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}
into

(n-1)\times 1\times(n-1)\times 1
components as

\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}=\begin{pmatrix}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}\\
\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{2}\\
\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}\\
\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{4}\end{pmatrix},\quad\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}=\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}\\
\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{2}\\
\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}\\
\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{4}\end{pmatrix},

and rewrite system (
3.22
) as follows

(3.23)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}+{\mathcal{S}}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1},

(3.24)

\displaystyle\lambda\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{2}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{2}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{2},

(3.25)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}-\langle\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}\rangle,

(3.26)

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{4}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{4}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{4}.

Observe that we have subtracted
{\langle{\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}}\rangle}
in (
3.25
)
in order to overcome the obstruction in the small divisors
cohomological equation—in
[
FMHM24
]
, we proved that

{\langle{\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}}\rangle}
is quadratically small so its contribution in
(
3.25
) is of second order. Then,

(3.27)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{2}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{\lambda 1}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{2},

(3.28)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}+{\langle{\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}}\rangle},

(3.29)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{4}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{\lambda^{\!\scalebox{0.5}{$-1$}}1}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{4},

where
{\langle{\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}}\rangle}
is free. We can then require

(3.30)

{\langle{\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}}\rangle}={\langle{{\mathcal{S}}}\rangle}^{\!-1}\left<\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}-{\mathcal{S}}{\mathfrak{R}}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}\right>

given the non-degeneracy condition
\det{\langle{{\mathcal{S}}}\rangle}\neq 0
.
Then

(3.31)

\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}={\mathfrak{R}}\bigg(\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}-{\mathcal{S}}\Big({\mathfrak{R}}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}+{\langle{\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{3}}\rangle}\Big)\bigg)+{\langle{\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}}\rangle},

where
{\langle{\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}^{1}}\rangle}
is free and we can simply take as zero.
This freedom has to do with the freedom to choose the phase of
the generating torus
{\mathcal{K}}
.

3.3.
One step on the bundle

For the parameterization of the invariant bundle,
we compute as well the correction in the coordinates given by the
frame
{P}
. That is, we take
{\Delta{W}}={P}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}
where

\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
satisfies

(3.32)

{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){P}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}-{P}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda-{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\Delta\lambda=-{\widetilde{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}},

and

(3.33)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}:

\displaystyle={{\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K+{\Delta K},\mathrm{id}){W}-{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda

\displaystyle={E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}+\int_{0}^{1}{{\rm D}^{2}_{z}}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}\big(K+s{\Delta K},\mathrm{id}\big)[{\Delta K},{W}]ds.

We emphasize that albeit we compute
{\Delta K}
in the previous
section, we linearize around
K
and not
\bar{K}=K+{\Delta K}
.
This allows us to use the same frame
{P}
to correct the torus
and the bundle. It is possible to consider only
terms up to first order in
{\Delta K}
in (
3.33
), i.e.,

(3.34)

{\widetilde{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}={E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}+{{\rm D}^{2}_{z}}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})[{\Delta K},{W}]

which can be easier to compute than (
3.33
) in
practical applications. However, for the proof on convergence,
(
3.34
) requires upper bounds on the norm of

{\rm D}^{3}_{z}\phi_{T}
, whereas (
3.33
) requires bounds only
of
{\rm D}^{2}_{z}\phi_{T}
.
It is also possible to compute first
\bar{K}
and consider
(
3.2
) around
\bar{K}
. Then, we can obtain
\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}
and
{\Delta\lambda}
from

{{\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(\bar{K},\mathrm{id})\bar{P}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}-\bar{P}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda-{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Delta\lambda}=-E_{\bar{K}{W}},

where
\bar{P}
is the frame for the corrected parameterization

\bar{K}
and
E_{\bar{K}{W}}
corresponds to (
3.20
) but on

\bar{K}
instead of
K
.
Notice that then we do not have to compute
{{\rm D}^{2}_{z}}\phi_{T}
but we
have to compute two frames: for
K
and for
\bar{K}
.
Computationally, this is an interesting option and it is in fact the
approach followed in
[
FMHM24
]
. However, for the KAM theorem,
it would require to control both frames
{P}
and
\bar{P}
.
In what follows, we consider only (
3.33
).

Let us left-multiply the linearized equation
(
3.32
) again by the approximate inverse of

{P}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}
, i.e., by
-{\Omega_{0}}{P}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}}\mspace{1.5mu}
, to obtain, after neglecting higher
order terms,

(3.35)

\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}\Lambda&S\\
\hline\cr&\raisebox{0.0pt}[9.90276pt][0.0pt]{$\Lambda^{-{\!\top\!}}$}\end{array}\right)\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}+e_{n}\Delta\lambda,

where

\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}:={\Omega_{0}}{P}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}}\mspace{1.5mu}{\widetilde{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}},

e_{n}:=\begin{pmatrix}0_{n-1}\\
1\\
0_{n-1}\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

Using analogous splittings for
\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}
and
\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}
as in Section

3.2.1
, system (
3.35
) reads

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{1}+{\mathcal{S}}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{3}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{1}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda

\displaystyle=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{1},

\displaystyle\lambda\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{2}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{2}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda

\displaystyle=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{2}+{\Delta\lambda},

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{3}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{3}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda

\displaystyle=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{3},

\displaystyle\lambda^{\!-1}\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{4}-\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{4}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda

\displaystyle=\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{4}.

We can then take
{\Delta\lambda}=-{\langle{\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{2}}\rangle}

and

(3.36)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{1}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{1\lambda}\big(\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{1}-{\mathcal{S}}{\mathfrak{R}}^{1\lambda}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{3}\big),

(3.37)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{R}}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{2}+{\langle{\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{2}}\rangle},

(3.38)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{3}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{1\lambda}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{3},

(3.39)

\displaystyle\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{4}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{\lambda^{\!-1}\lambda}\eta_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{4},

where
{\langle{\xi_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}^{2}}\rangle}
is free and we can take as zero.
This freedom has to do with the liberty to choose the size of the
frame generating the stable bundle of
{\mathcal{K}}
.

3.4.
The KAM theorem

The convergence of the iterative process described in Section

3.2
leads to an
a-posteriori
KAM theorem
on existence of partially hyperbolic invariant tori and its
invariant bundles. In what follows, recall the definitions for
the sets
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}
from Section
2.3
.

Theorem 3.4

(KAM theorem for partially hyperbolic tori)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\omega}={\rm d}\boldsymbol{\alpha}
be an exact symplectic form defined in an open set

U\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
and let
\boldsymbol{J}
be an almost complex structure
on
U
compatible with
\boldsymbol{\omega}
. Let
\boldsymbol{g}
be the induced metric
and
H:U\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a real-analytic quasi-periodic Hamiltonian
with frequencies
\hat{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
. For
d=n-2
, let

\omega\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},~T>0,
and
\alpha=\hat{\alpha}T
be such that

(\omega,\alpha)
is Diophantine with constants
\gamma>0,

\tau\geq d+\ell
and suppose we have
K:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to U,~{W}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
, and
|{\lambda}|<1
. Assume the
following hypothesis.

H_{1}

There exists some
r,R>0

such that the geometric objects
\boldsymbol{\omega},~\boldsymbol{\alpha},~\boldsymbol{J},~
and
\boldsymbol{g}

can be analytically extended to the complex set

{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{2n}
containing
U
, see (
2.3
). Moreover, for all

t\in[0,T]
and all
(z,\varphi)\in{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}
,
\tilde{\phi}_{t}(z,\varphi)
exists.
Then, there exist constants such that:

–

the matrix representations
\Omega,~a,~{J},
and
G

satisfy

\displaystyle\|{\Omega}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\Omega$}},

\displaystyle\|{{\rm D}\Omega}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\Omega}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{{\rm D}a}}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}a}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{({\rm D}a)^{\!\top}}}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$({{\rm D}a})^{\!\top\!}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{{\rm D}^{2}\!a}}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}^{2}\!a}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{J}}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${J}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{J}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${J}^{\!\top\!}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{\rm D}{J}}\|_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\rm D}{J}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{(\rm D}{J})^{\!\top\!}}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${(\rm D}{J})^{\!\top\!}$}},

\displaystyle\|{G}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}

\displaystyle\leq{c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$G$}}},

\displaystyle\|{{\rm D}G}\|_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\rm D}G$}}.

–

The Hamiltonian
H
, the vector field
X
, and the flow
map
\phi_{T}
satisfy

\displaystyle\|{X}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$X$}},

\displaystyle\|{X^{\!\top\!}}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$J^{\!\top\!}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{\rm D}_{z}X}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\rm D}_{z}X$}},

\displaystyle\|{({\rm D}_{z}X)^{\!\top\!}}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$({\rm D}_{z}X)^{\!\top\!}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{\rm D}^{2}_{z}H}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}

\displaystyle\leq{c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\rm D}^{2}_{z}H$}}},

\displaystyle\|{{{\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T}}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${{\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T}$}}},

\displaystyle\|{{({\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T})^{\!\top\!}}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${({\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T})^{\!\top\!}$}}},

\displaystyle\|{{{\rm D}^{2}_{z}}\phi_{T}}\|_{{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${{\rm D}^{2}_{z}}\phi_{T}$}}}.

H_{2}

There exists
\rho\in(0,r)
such that

K\in{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})^{2n}
,
{\rm D}K\in{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})^{2n\times(d+\ell)}
, and

{W}\in{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho})^{2n}
.

–

Moreover, there exist constants such that

\displaystyle\|{{\rm D}_{\theta}K}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${\rm D}_{\theta}K$}}},

\displaystyle\|{({{\rm D}_{\theta}K})^{{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\left({\rm D}_{\theta}K\right)^{\!\top\!}$}}},

\displaystyle\|{{\rm D}_{\varphi}K}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${\rm D}_{\varphi}K$}}},

\displaystyle\|{({{\rm D}_{\varphi}K})^{{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\left({\rm D}_{\varphi}K\right)^{\!\top\!}$}}},

\displaystyle\|{{W}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${W}$}}},

\displaystyle\|{{W^{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${W}^{\!\top\!}$}}},

\displaystyle\|{\hat{N}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}$}},

\displaystyle\|{\hat{N}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}^{\!\top\!}$}},

\displaystyle\|{{B}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<{\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$B$}}}.

–

Also, there exist
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\lambda$}}}<1
and

\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\lambda^{\scalebox{0.7}{$-1$}}$}}}>1
satisfying

|{\lambda}|<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\lambda$}}},\ |{\lambda^{\!-1}}|<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\lambda^{\scalebox{0.7}{$-1$}}$}}}.

H_{3}

There exists a constant
\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\langle{{\mathcal{S}}}\rangle}^{\!-1}$}}
such that the shear

{\mathcal{S}}
in (
3.9
), which coincides with the
block
\hat{S}_{1}
in
(
3.12
), satisfies the non-degeneracy condition

\big|{\langle{{\mathcal{S}}}\rangle}^{-1}\big|<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\langle{{\mathcal{S}}}\rangle}^{\!-1}$}}
.

Then, for every
\rho_{\infty}\in(0,\rho)
and

\delta\in(0,\frac{\rho-\rho_{\infty}}{3})
, there exist a constant

\mathfrak{C}
such that if the invariance errors

\displaystyle{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}=\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})-K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu},

\displaystyle{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}={\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){W}-{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda,

satisfy

(3.40)

\frac{\mathfrak{C}}{\gamma^{2}\delta^{2\tau}}{\mathcal{E}}<1,

where

(3.41)

{\mathcal{E}}:={\max{\left\{\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{\gamma^{2}\delta^{2\tau}},\ \|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho}\right\}}},

then there exist
\lambda_{\infty}
, an invariant torus

{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}=K_{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell})

and an invariant bundle
{\mathcal{W}}_{\infty}={W}_{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell})

satisfying:

i)

K_{\infty},{W}_{\infty}\in{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho_{\infty}})^{2n}
and

K_{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho_{\infty}})\subset{\mathcal{U}}
.

ii)

K_{\infty},{W}_{\infty}
, and the associated objects

\hat{N}_{\infty},{B}_{\infty},
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\infty}
satisfy
H_{2}
and

H_{3}
in the complex strip
{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}_{\rho_{\infty}}
.

iii)

The dynamics on
{\mathcal{K}}_{\infty}
are conjugated to a rigid
rotation with rotation vector
(\omega,\alpha)
and on

{\mathcal{W}}_{\infty}
to a constant contraction with rate

|{\lambda_{\infty}}|
.

iv)

The limiting objects are close to the original ones in
the following sense

(3.42)

\displaystyle\|{K_{\infty}-K}\|_{\rho_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{C}_{\Delta K}{\mathcal{E}},

(3.43)

\displaystyle\|{{W}_{\infty}-{W}}\|_{\rho_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathfrak{C}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\Delta{W}}$}}}{\gamma\delta^{\tau}}{\mathcal{E}},

(3.44)

\displaystyle|{\lambda_{\infty}-\lambda}|

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{C}_{{\Delta\lambda}}{\mathcal{E}},

(3.45)

\displaystyle|{\lambda^{\!-1}_{\infty}-\lambda^{\!-1}}|

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{C}_{{\Delta\lambda^{\!-1}}}{\mathcal{E}},

for some explicit constants
\mathfrak{C}_{\Delta K},\mathfrak{C}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\Delta{W}}$}},\mathfrak{C}_{{\Delta\lambda}}
, and
\mathfrak{C}_{{\Delta\lambda^{\!-1}}}
.

Remark 3.5
.

In hypothesis
H_{2}
, it is not strictly necessary to assume
bounds for
\hat{N}
since we can control them
automatically from the bounds on
{J},{L},
and
{B}
.
Nonetheless, we include it in the hypothesis for the
sake of flexibility in order apply the theorem to particular
problems.

Remark 3.6
.

Note that, since we use the matrix-valued map
{A}
to reduce the
torsion to the form (
3.9
), we obtain a
parameterization
\widetilde{W}_{\infty}
of another invariant bundle

\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\infty}
satisfying (
3.10
).

Remark 3.7
.

The theorem applies to autonomous systems with minor
modifications—it only requires setting to zero some quantities.

4.
Error estimates of approximate geometric
properties

For the proof of Theorem
3.4
, we first need to control
certain geometric properties that only hold
approximately when the parameterizations of tori and bundles satisfy
their invariance equations approximately. In this section we
obtain upper bounds for these properties in terms of the error in
the invariance equations.

4.1.
Adapted frames control

Let us begin by controlling the adapted frame
we use throughout the iterative scheme. Let

(4.1)

\displaystyle{C_{{\mathcal{X}}}}

\displaystyle:=c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$X$}}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${\rm D}_{\varphi}K$}}}|{\hat{\alpha}}|,

\displaystyle{C_{{{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}}}}

\displaystyle:=c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$J^{\!\top\!}$}}+|{\hat{\alpha}^{\!\top\!}}|\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\left({\rm D}_{\varphi}K\right)^{\!\top\!}$}}},

(4.2)

\displaystyle{C_{{L}}}

\displaystyle:=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${\rm D}_{\theta}K$}}}+{C_{{\mathcal{X}}}}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${W}$}}},

\displaystyle{C_{{{L}}^{\!\top\!}}}

\displaystyle:={\max{\left\{\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\left({\rm D}_{\theta}K\right)^{\!\top\!}$}}},{C_{{{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}}}},\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${W}^{\!\top\!}$}}}\right\}}}.

Then,

\displaystyle\|{{\mathcal{X}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<{C_{{\mathcal{X}}}},

\displaystyle\|{{{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<{C_{{{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}}}},

\displaystyle\|{{L}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<{C_{{L}}},

\displaystyle\|{{{L}}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<{C_{{{L}}^{\!\top\!}}}.

If we define
C_{\hat{P}}:={C_{{L}}}+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}$}}
and
C_{{\hat{P}}^{\!\top\!}}:={\max{\left\{{C_{{{L}}^{\!\top\!}}},\ \sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}^{\!\top\!}$}}\right\}}}
, we can control the frame
{\hat{P}}
as

(4.3)

\|{{\hat{P}}}\|_{\rho}\leq C_{\hat{P}},\qquad\|{{\hat{P}}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}\leq C_{{\hat{P}}^{\!\top\!}}.

Similarly, let
{C_{\hat{S}}}:=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}^{\!\top\!}$}}c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\Omega$}}c_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${{\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T}$}}}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}$}}

and
{C_{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}}:=\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}^{\!\top\!}$}}c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\Omega$}}c_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${({\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T})^{\!\top\!}$}}}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}$}}
. Hence we have
the following estimates for
\hat{S}
and
\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}

(4.4)

\|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}<{C_{\hat{S}}},\qquad\|{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}<{C_{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}}.

In order to control the frame
{P}
,
let us first obtain estimates for
\|{{A}}\|_{\rho}
and

\|{{A^{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}
as follows

\displaystyle\|{A}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq{\max{\left\{\|{A_{2}}\|_{\rho},\ \|{A_{3}}\|_{\rho}+\|{A_{4}}\|_{\rho}\right\}}},

\displaystyle\|{{A^{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq{\max{\left\{\|{{A}_{3}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho},\ \|{{A}_{2}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}+\|{A_{4}}\|_{\rho}\right\}}}.

Note that
A_{2}
and
A_{3}
satisfy (
3.17
)
and (
3.18
), respectively. Therefore, we can use
estimate (
2.8
) and obtain

\displaystyle\|{A_{2}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}\left(\|{\hat{S}_{2}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda}|+\|{\hat{S}_{3}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|\right),

\displaystyle\|{A_{3}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}\left(\|{\hat{S}_{3}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}_{2}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}\right),

\displaystyle\|{A_{2}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}\left(\|{\hat{S}_{2}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda}|+\|{\hat{S}_{3}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|\right),

\displaystyle\|{A_{3}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}\left(\|{\hat{S}_{3}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}_{2}}\|_{\rho}\right).

Also,
A_{4}
satisfies (
3.16
) so

\|{A_{4}}\|_{\rho}\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}\|{\hat{S}_{4}}\|_{\rho}.

Then,

\displaystyle\|{{A}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}{\max{\left\{\|{\hat{S}_{2}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda}|+\|{\hat{S}_{3}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|,\ \|{\hat{S}_{3}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}_{2}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}_{4}}\|_{\rho}\right\}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}{\max{\left\{\|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}_{3}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|,\ \|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}_{2}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|\right\}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}\left(\|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-|{\lambda}|^{2}}\left(\|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda}|+\|{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}\right).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\|{{A}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}{\max{\left\{\|{\hat{S}_{3}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}_{2}}\|_{\rho},\ \|{\hat{S}_{2}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda}|+\|{\hat{S}_{3}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|+\|{\hat{S}_{4}}\|_{\rho}\right\}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}{\max{\left\{\|{\hat{S}_{3}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|,\ \|{\hat{S}_{2}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|\right\}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|-|{\lambda}|}\left(\|{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}+\|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda^{\!-1}}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-|{\lambda}|^{2}}\left(\|{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}|{\lambda}|+\|{\hat{S}}\|_{\rho}\right).

Finally we obtain

(4.5)

\displaystyle\|{A}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{1-(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\lambda$}}})^{2}}\left({C_{\hat{S}}}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\lambda$}}}+{C_{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}}\right)=:{C_{{A}}},

(4.6)

\displaystyle\|{{A^{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{1-(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\lambda$}}})^{2}}\left({C_{\hat{S}^{\!\top\!}}}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\lambda$}}}+{C_{\hat{S}}}\right)=:{C_{{A^{\!\top\!}}}}.

We can now control
{N}
and
{{N}}^{\!\top\!}
as

(4.7)

\displaystyle\|{{N}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\hat{N}}\|_{\rho}+\|{{L}}\|_{\rho}\|{{A}}\|_{\rho}<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}$}}+{C_{{L}}}{C_{{A}}}=:{C_{{N}}},

(4.8)

\displaystyle\|{{{N}}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\hat{N}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}+\|{{A^{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}\|{L^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho}<\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\hat{N}^{\!\top\!}$}}+{C_{{A^{\!\top\!}}}}{C_{{{L}}^{\!\top\!}}}=:{C_{{{N}}^{\!\top\!}}},

and the frame
{P}
as

(4.9)

\displaystyle\|{{P}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<{C_{{L}}}+{C_{{N}}}=:C_{P},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\|{{P^{\!\top\!}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle<{\max{\left\{{C_{{{L}}^{\!\top\!}}},\ {C_{{{N}}^{\!\top\!}}}\right\}}}=:C_{{P}^{\!\top\!}}.

4.2.
Approximate invariance of the subframe
{L}

Let
E_{L}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times n}
denote the error in the
invariance of the subframe
{L}
. That is,

(4.11)

E_{L}:={\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){L}-{L}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\Lambda.

Also, let us define

\displaystyle\Delta X:=

\displaystyle X{\!\>\circ\!\>}\big(\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}),{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\big)-X{\!\>\circ\!\>}\left(K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu},{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}{\rm D}_{z}X{\!\>\circ\!\>}\big(K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+s{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}},{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\big){E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}ds,

where the last equality only holds in
{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}

for sufficiently small
{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}
. More specifically, suppose we want to obtain upper
bounds for
\|{\Delta X}\|_{\rho-\delta}
. Then, we need that
for all
({\theta,\varphi})\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}_{\rho-\delta}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}_{\rho-\delta}

\left(K({\bar{\theta},\bar{\varphi}})+s{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}({\theta,\varphi}),\bar{\varphi}\right)\in{\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}.

Equivalently, for all
({\theta,\varphi})\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}_{\rho-\delta}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}_{\rho-\delta}

and all
s\in[0,1]
, we need some

\theta^{*}\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}_{\rho-\delta}
such that

\left|{K({\bar{\theta},\bar{\varphi}})+s{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}({\theta,\varphi})-{K_{0}}(\theta^{*},\bar{\varphi})}\right|<R.

Observe that if

(4.12)

\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{R-\|{K-{K_{0}}}\|_{\rho}}<1

holds, we can take
\theta^{*}=\bar{\theta}
and,
consequently,
\Delta X
is well-defined in

{\mathbb{T}}^{d}_{\rho-\delta}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{\ell}_{\rho-\delta}
.
We will encounter the need of hypothesis (
4.12
) in
order to guarantee that analogous norms for various objects are well
defined. Therefore, in what follows, we assume (
4.12
)
holds.

Lemma 4.1
.

The error in the invariance of the subframe
{L}

is given by

(4.13)

E_{L}=\left({\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}\ \big|\ E_{\mathcal{X}}\ \big|\ {E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}\right),

where

E_{\mathcal{X}}:=\Delta X-{\rm D}_{\varphi}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}\hat{\alpha}.

Additionally, for any
\delta\in(0,\rho)

(4.14)

\displaystyle\|{E_{L}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{L}$}}^{K}}}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}+{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{L}$}}^{W}}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho},

(4.15)

\displaystyle\|{E_{L}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{L}^{\!\top\!}$}}^{K}}}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}+{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{L}^{\!\top\!}$}}^{W}}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho}.

Proof.

Let us first differentiate (
3.19
) with respect to both
\theta
and
\varphi
to
obtain

(4.16)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}

\displaystyle={\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\rm D}_{\theta}K-{\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu},

(4.17)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{\varphi}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}

\displaystyle={\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\rm D}_{\varphi}K+{\rm D}_{\varphi}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})-{\rm D}_{\varphi}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}.

The first term in (
4.13
) is immediate from (
4.16
).
Also, the last term in (
4.13
) is precisely definition
(
3.20
).
For the second term, we need to show that

{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}=E_{\mathcal{X}}.

First observe that at
t=0

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left(\tilde{\phi}_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}\tilde{\phi}_{t}(z,\varphi)\right)=\begin{pmatrix}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}(z,\varphi)&{\rm D}_{\varphi}\phi_{T}(z,\varphi)\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{\ell\times 2n}&I_{\ell}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}X(x,\varphi)\\
\hat{\alpha}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}X\big(\phi_{T}(z,\varphi),\bar{\varphi}\big)\\
\hat{\alpha}\end{pmatrix}.

From the first block row, at
z=K({\theta,\varphi})
, we
obtain

{{\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T}\big(K({\theta,\varphi}),\varphi\big)X\big(K({\theta,\varphi}),\varphi\big)+{\rm D}_{\varphi}\phi_{T}\big(K({\theta,\varphi}),\varphi\big)\hat{\alpha}=X\Big(\phi_{T}\big(K({\theta,\varphi}),\varphi\big),\bar{\varphi}\Big).

Then, after using (
4.17
) and some manipulation, we
finally obtain

{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}=\Delta X-{\rm D}_{\varphi}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}\hat{\alpha}.

For the estimate (
4.14
), note that for any
\delta\in(0,\rho)

\|{E_{\mathcal{X}}}\|_{\rho-\delta}\leq\|{\Delta X}\|_{\rho-\delta}+\|{{\rm D}_{\varphi}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho-\delta}|{\hat{\alpha}}|

and from assumption (
4.12
),
\Delta X
is well-defined.
Then, we can obtain the estimates

(4.18)

\displaystyle\|{E_{\mathcal{X}}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\left(c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\rm D}_{z}X$}}\delta+\ell|{\hat{\alpha}}|\right)\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{\delta}\leq C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{\mathcal{X}}$}}\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{\delta},

(4.19)

\displaystyle\|{E_{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\left(\Delta X\right)^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho-\delta}+\left|{\hat{\alpha}^{\!\top\!}}\right|\|{\left({\rm D}_{\varphi}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}\right)^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq 2n\left(c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$({\rm D}_{z}X)^{\!\top\!}$}}\delta+\left|{\hat{\alpha}^{\!\top\!}}\right|\right)\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{\delta}\leq C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}$}}\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{\delta}.

Lastly,

\displaystyle\|{E_{L}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\|{{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho-\delta}+\|{E_{\mathcal{X}}}\|_{\rho-\delta}+\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}+\frac{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{\mathcal{X}}$}}}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}+\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(d+C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{\mathcal{X}}$}}\right)}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}+\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{L}$}}^{K}}}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}+{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{L}$}}^{W}}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho},

\displaystyle\|{E_{L}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq{\max{\left\{\|{\left({\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}\right)^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho-\delta},\ \|{E_{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho-\delta},\ \|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}^{\!\top\!}}\|_{\rho-\delta}\right\}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\max{\left\{\frac{2n}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho},\ \frac{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}$}}}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho},\ 2n\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho}\right\}}}

\displaystyle\leq{\max{\left\{2n,\ C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}$}}\right\}}}\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{\delta}+2n\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{L}^{\!\top\!}$}}^{K}}}{\delta}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}+{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{$E_{L}^{\!\top\!}$}}^{W}}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}}\|_{\rho}.

∎

4.3.
Approximate isotropy of
{\mathcal{K}}

For each
\varphi\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, let us denote the coordinate
representation of the pullback of
the symplectic form
\boldsymbol{\omega}
by
K
, as

{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times 2n}
given by

{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}:=({\rm D}_{\theta}K)^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}K.

Let us also define

(4.20)

\displaystyle{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}:=

\displaystyle\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})-\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}{\rm D}\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+s{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}){E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}ds,

where, similarly as in Section
4.2
, the last equality
only holds in
{\mathcal{U}}
for sufficiently small
{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

The approximate isotropy of
{\mathcal{K}}
is given by

{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}={\mathfrak{R}}\left({\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}\right)
,
where

(4.21)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle={\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}-{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}

\displaystyle=({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{\!\top\!}{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}{\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+({\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}})^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\rm D}_{\theta}K.

Additionally, for any
\delta\in(0,\rho)

(4.22)

\|{{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}}\|_{\rho-\delta}\leq\frac{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{${\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}$}}}{\gamma\delta^{\tau+1}}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}.

Proof.

From the symplecticity of
\phi_{T}
and (
4.16
)

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}=

\displaystyle({\rm D}_{\theta}K)^{\!\top\!}\big({\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})\big)^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\rm D}_{\theta}K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}\Big)^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}\Big({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}

\displaystyle+({\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}})^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\rm D}_{\theta}K.

Then, using the previous expression, the definition of

{\mathfrak{L}}
, and
(
4.20
)
we obtain (
4.21
).
We also need to show that
{\langle{{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}}\rangle}=0
. Observe that
since
\Omega=({\rm D}a)^{\!\top\!}-{\rm D}a

{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}=\big({\rm D}_{\theta}(a{\!\>\circ\!\>}K)\big)^{\!\top\!}{\rm D}_{\theta}K-\big({\rm D}_{\theta}K\big)^{\!\top\!}{\rm D}_{\theta}(a{\!\>\circ\!\>}K)

and each entry
ij
of
{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}
reads

\displaystyle({\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}})_{ij}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\partial_{\theta^{i}}\big(a_{k}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K)\big)\partial_{\theta^{j}}K^{k}-\partial_{\theta^{j}}\big(a_{k}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K\big)\partial_{\theta^{i}}K^{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\partial_{\theta^{i}}\big(a_{k}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K\partial_{\theta^{j}}K^{k}\big)-a_{k}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K\partial^{2}_{\theta^{i}\theta^{j}}K^{k}

\displaystyle-\partial_{\theta^{j}}\big(a_{k}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K\partial_{\theta^{i}}K^{k}\big)+a_{k}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K\partial^{2}_{\theta^{i}\theta^{j}}K^{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\partial_{\theta^{i}}\big(a_{k}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K\partial_{\theta^{j}}K^{k}\big)-\partial_{\theta^{j}}\big(a_{k}{\!\>\circ\!\>}K\partial_{\theta^{i}}K^{k}\big).

Then,
{\langle{{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}}\rangle}=0
since we are taking averages of
derivatives with respect to
\theta
. Therefore,
{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}={\mathfrak{R}}\left({\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}\right)
.
For the estimate (
4.22
), note first that—similarly
as in Lemma
4.1
—
{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}
is
well-defined by assumption (
4.12
). Then, we can obtain the
following estimate from (
4.21
)

(4.23)

\displaystyle\|{{\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}}\|_{\rho-\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\left({\rm D}_{\theta}K\right)^{\!\top\!}$}}}c_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\Omega}$}}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${\rm D}_{\theta}K$}}}\delta+\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{$\left({\rm D}_{\theta}K\right)^{\!\top\!}$}}}c_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\Omega$}}d+2nc_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\Omega$}}c_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${{\rm D}_{z}}\phi_{T}$}}}\sigma_{\scalebox{0.65}{{${\rm D}_{\theta}K$}}}\Big)\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{${\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}$}}}\frac{\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}}{\delta},

and, using the Rüssmann estimate (
2.10
), we obtain

\|{{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}}\|_{\rho-2\delta}\leq\frac{c_{\mathfrak{R}}{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{${\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}$}}}}{\gamma\delta^{\tau+1}}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}\leq\frac{C_{\scalebox{0.75}{${\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}$}}}{\gamma\delta^{\tau+1}}\|{{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}}\|_{\rho}.

∎

4.4.
Approximate Lagrangianity of the subframe
L

The Lagrangianity of the subframe
L
is given by

{\Omega_{L}}:{\mathbb{T}}^{d+\ell}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
constructed as

{\Omega_{L}}:=L^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}L=\begin{pmatrix}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}&{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}&{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{W}$}}}\\
{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}&{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}&{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{W}$}}}\\
{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}&{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}&{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}\end{pmatrix},

where

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=({\rm D}_{\theta}K)^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}K,

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}

\displaystyle:=({\rm D}_{\theta}K)^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{\mathcal{X}},

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{W}$}}}

\displaystyle:=({\rm D}_{\theta}K)^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{W},

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle:={\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}K,

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}

\displaystyle:={\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{\mathcal{X}},

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{W}$}}}

\displaystyle:={\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{W},

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle:={W}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}K,

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}

\displaystyle:={W}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{\mathcal{X}},

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}

\displaystyle:={W}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}K}\mspace{1.5mu}{W}.

Observe that because
\Omega
is skew-symmetric, we have

{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}=-{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}^{\!\top\!}
,
{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}=-{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{W}$}}}^{\!\top\!}
, and

{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}=-{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{W}$}}}^{\!\top\!}
. Furthermore,
that using the symplecticity of
\phi_{T}
, (
4.11
), and
definition (
4.20
), we obtain that
{\Omega_{L}}
is a solution of
the following equation

(4.24)

\displaystyle{\Omega_{L}}-{\Lambda^{\!\top\!}}{\Omega_{L}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\Lambda=

\displaystyle{\Lambda^{\!\top\!}}{L}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}{L}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\Lambda

\displaystyle+{\Lambda^{\!\top\!}}{L}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}E_{L}

\displaystyle+E_{L}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){L}.

Lemma 4.3
.

The approximate Lagrangianity of the frame
L
,
is given by

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}$}}}\big),

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}\big),

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{W}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{1\lambda}\big({\mathfrak{L}}^{1\lambda}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{W}$}}}\big),

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}\big({\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}\big),

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{W}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{1\lambda}\big({\mathfrak{L}}^{1\lambda}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{W}$}}}\big),

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{1\lambda}\big({\mathfrak{L}}^{1\lambda}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}\big),

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{R}}^{1\lambda}\big({\mathfrak{L}}^{1\lambda}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}\big),

\displaystyle{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}

\displaystyle=0,

where

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{\mathcal{X}}$}}}

\displaystyle=({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{\!\top\!}{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}{\mathcal{X}}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}E_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\mathcal{X}},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{1\lambda}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${{\rm D}\!K}\!{W}$}}}

\displaystyle=({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{\!\top\!}{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda+({\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu})^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){W},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}{\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+E_{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\rm D}_{\theta}K,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{1\lambda}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}{W}$}}}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}{W}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}\lambda+{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+E_{\mathcal{X}}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){W},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{L}}^{1\lambda}{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}{{\rm D}\!K}$}}}

\displaystyle=\lambda{W}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Delta\Omega\hskip 0.86108pt}{\rm D}_{\theta}K{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}+\lambda{W}^{\!\top\!}{\!\>\circ\!\>}{{\mathcal{R}}}_{\omega\alpha}\mspace{1.5mu}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{\theta}{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{$K$}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+{E_{\scalebox{0.65}{${W}$}}}^{\!\top\!}{\Omega{\!\>\circ\!\>}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id})}\mspace{1.5mu}{\rm D}_{z}\phi_{T}{\!\>\circ\!\>}(K,\mathrm{id}){\rm D}_{\theta}K,
